# Grammar §4 formalisation — headline index (general-dimensional normal block)

## Programme Q — after Programme P, resolution deferred (opened 2026-09-09; Astra #38; cap 24 units; reviews after N1, N3, the N2 gate, release)
User (2026-09-09): "don't worry about resolution of singularities for now, but keep working on the rest". Astra #38 (`tide-log/gpt6_bigpicture_v38.md`): N1 first-nonzero selection after cancellation + superpolynomial flatness (6); N3 analytic-family → `TangentialData` admissibility on a common polydisc (8); N2 exact chart energy identity `𝒵_K = −(1/β)𝒵'` + first log correction by coefficient algebra, Route B, gate after 2 units (7); N4 positive-box dilation and coefficient transport (3); reserves N8; deferred N5 parity, N6 wall-crossing; N7 all-orders division still NO-GO.
**N1 — first nonzero term after cancellation; flatness — COMPLETE (units 306–308).**
| Headline | Statement | File:line |
|---|---|---|
| — | **first nonzero pair (u306)**: `admissible D Q = {p : p.1 ∈ Q⁻¹ℕ, p.2 ≤ D}`; a finite set with a nonzero coefficient has a first nonzero pair in the order `precedes` (min exponent, then max log degree; `exists_first_nonzero_finset`); the index set below a cutoff contains every admissible predecessor of its members (`indexSet_pred_closed`), so a nonzero coefficient below SOME cutoff gives a globally first nonzero admissible pair (`exists_first_nonzero`), unique (`first_nonzero_unique`, `existsUnique_first_nonzero`), with vanishing predecessor sum (`absPredSum_eq_zero_of_first`); regression: at a fixed exponent the larger log degree wins (`first_nonzero_snd_ge`) | FirstNonzero.lean |
| ★ | **the first nonzero term is the leading term (u307)**: for any `CutoffExpansion Q D Z c`, `Z ~ c_p N^{-p.1}(log N)^{p.2}` at the first nonzero admissible pair `p` (`isEquivalent_first_nonzero_of_cutoffExpansion`; via `tendsto_abstractRemainder`), unique pair (`existsUnique_first_nonzero_isEquivalent`); the population Taylor tree on the unit box is a `CutoffExpansion` (`cutoffExpansion_of_conclusion`); chart level `population_first_nonzero_chart`; assembled `population_first_nonzero_assembled` with the residual negligible at EVERY admissible scale (`_exp`: exponentially small) — negligibility at Programme P's first candidate is not enough after a cancellation | FirstNonzeroAsymptotic.lean |
| — | **superpolynomial flatness (u308)**: all admissible coefficients zero ⇒ `Z(N)/N^{-L} → 0` for every real `L` (`flat_of_cutoffExpansion`); the assembled integral of any joint datum is a `CutoffExpansion` (`cutoffExpansion_gInt`, from `gCutoff_bound`); assembled flatness `population_flat`, `_exp`; regression: `e^{-N}` is flat and never zero (`exp_neg_flat`, `exp_neg_ne_zero`) — flat does not mean zero | Flatness.lean |

**N3 — analytic amplitude families are admissible tangential data — COMPLETE (units 309–312).**

| ★ | **analytic families are admissible zero-noise data (u311)**: for `1 < r < R`, `F x` holomorphic on the polydisc of radius `R`, `(x,w) ↦ F x w` continuous and bounded by `M` on `X × closedPolydisc r`: `analyticDatum F x = ofFamilies 1 0 (polyRealCoeff d r (F x))` has `xiCoord = 0`, `etaCoord = polyRealCoeff`, is continuous into `DataSpace d` (`continuous_analyticDatum`; geometric majorant `M r^{-|γ|}`, summable as `r > 1`), and its represented amplitude is `Re (F x)` on the closed cube (`dataAmplitude_analyticDatum`, reconstruction `evalF_polyRealCoeff_of_lt`); on compact `K` this is `analyticTangential F : TangentialData K d` with zero noise | AnalyticFamilyData.lean |

Non-claims (N3): the common holomorphic neighbourhood with a strict radius gap and the tangential-only cutoff are hypotheses (no construction); chart maps and Jacobians are external; the uniform bound `M` is a hypothesis (automatic from compactness and joint continuity but not derived here).

**N2 — the energy observable: exact identity, coefficient transport and the first logarithmic correction — COMPLETE AT CHART LEVEL (units 313–316; gate review v39, release review v40). The assembled extension was omitted: what is missing is the paired numerator/denominator assembled coefficient identification and the assembly/tangential-integration bridges at the next-log scale, not residual decay (the assembled hypotheses already assert residuals at every scale).**
| Headline | Statement | File:line |
|---|---|---|
| ★ | **exact energy identity (u313)**: for continuous `η` and `N₀ > 0`, `d/dN ∫ η u^h e^{-βN u^{2k}} = −β ∫ (u^{2k} η) u^h e^{-βN u^{2k}}` (`hasDerivAt_popIntegral`, differentiation under the integral, dominated by `β|η|u^h u^{2k}` on `|N−N₀| < N₀/2`); `𝒵_N[K∘π] = −(1/β) 𝒵_N'[1]` (`energy_eq_neg_deriv`; the paper's `𝒵_n[K] = −𝒵_n'(n)` at `β = 1`) — an identity of integrals, never used to differentiate an expansion | EnergyDerivative.lean |
| — | **moment recurrence (u314)**: `M(μ+1, i) = (μ M(μ,i) − i M(μ,i−1))/β` for `μ, β > 0` (`fluctMoment_succ_exponent`; integration by parts on `(0,∞)` with `u = t^μ(−log t)^i`, boundary terms vanish by `t^μ(−log t)^i e^{−βt} → 0` at both ends) | MomentRecurrence.lean |
| — | **state-density shift (u315a)**: `conv (w+1) (shift 1 c) = shift 1 (conv w c)`, `stateDensityRep n (w+1) = shift 1 (stateDensityRep n w)`, `coeffAt (shift 1 c) (μ+1) q = coeffAt c μ q`, `monoWeights (h+2k) k = monoWeights h k + 1` | PowLogShift.lean |
| ★ | **the first logarithmic energy correction (u316; `ex:phi_equals_K` second term)**: at chart level, for continuous `η` with a holomorphic extension (`R > 1`), `λ`, `m`, and `A = amplitudeCoeff h k λ β η ≠ 0`: `N log N · (𝒵_N[K∘π]/𝒵_N[1] − λ/(βN)) → −(m−1)/β` for every `m ≥ 1` (`energy_correction_chart`; `β = 1`: `E_n[K] = λ/n − (m−1)/(n log n) + o(1/(n log n))`). Route B: two-term expansions from the remainder at every log scale (`population_remainder_tendsto_pow`, `_mul_pow`) and the split at the top two log degrees (`twoTerm_split`), coefficients `A_K = λA/β`, `B_K = (λB − (m−1)A)/β` from the transport, quotient algebra `twoTerm_energy_quotient` (`m ≥ 2`) / `oneTerm_energy_quotient` (`m = 1`); nothing differentiated asymptotically | EnergyCorrection.lean, EnergyCorrectionCore.lean |
| ★ | **coefficient transport (u315)**: `C_K(μ+1, j) = (μ C(μ,j) − (j+1) C(μ,j+1))/β` for every `μ > 0`, `j` (`population_coeff_add_two_k`; kernel level `kernelS_add_two_k`, `kernelFunctional_add_two_k` via `C(q,j)(q−j) = (j+1)C(q,j+1)`); coefficients vanish above the top log degree (`population_coeff_eq_zero_of_gt_degree`). At `(λ, m−1)`: `A_K = (λ/β)A` (u304 recovered); at `(λ, m−2)`: `B_K = (λB − (m−1)A)/β` — Route B of Astra #38, coefficient algebra only | CoefficientTransport.lean |

**N4 — positive normal boxes — COMPLETE (units 317–318; review v40 PASS, u318 qualified PASS: leading coefficient only).**

| — | **box coefficient transport (u318)**: the box Taylor tree's coefficient system is the unit-box system of the rescaled family, so `C(λ,j) = 0` above `m−1` and `C(λ,m−1) = amplitudeCoeff h k λ β (η(b·))`; the paper-facing box coefficient `boxCoeff … b … λ (m−1) = b^H (b^{2K})^{-λ} amplitudeCoeff h k λ β (η(b·))` (final clause of `population_box_leadingCoeff`; vanishing proved for `m−1 < j ≤ n`); lower log coefficients are `boxCoeff`'s binomial re-expansion of `log N + 2K log b` (positive shift, audited v40), no closed form claimed | BoxCoefficientTransport.lean |

**N8 (reserve) — full-data, coefficient-level zero-noise continuity corollaries (unit 319; review v40 PASS).**
| Headline | Statement | File:line |
|---|---|---|
| — | **coefficients continuous in the data**: `X a → x` in the weighted-ℓ¹ data topology implies `dataBoxCoeff (X a) μ j → dataBoxCoeff x μ j` for every `(μ, j)` (`tendsto_dataBoxCoeff_of_tendsto`, from Headline XXXIV); **population limit at the first candidate**: if the limit datum has zero noise, the `(λ, m−1)` coefficient converges to `amplitudeCoeff h k λ β (dataAmplitude x)` (`tendsto_dataBoxCoeff_leading`). Non-claims: convergence of individual coefficients, not of expansions; normalised empirical noise need not tend to zero; nothing stochastic | ZeroNoiseContinuity.lean |

**Programme Q release (u306–319; 14 of 24 units; reviews v37–v40 PASS; release audit v40 RELEASE after doc fixes).** Completed first-nonzero selection and superpolynomial flatness (N1); analytic-family admissibility and the tangential integral bridge under a common holomorphic neighbourhood (N3); the exact chart energy derivative identity and coefficient transport, and the first logarithmic energy correction `N log N (𝒵_K/𝒵 − λ/(βN)) → −(m−1)/β` for analytic chart amplitudes with nonzero face coefficient, including multiplicity one (N2, chart level); positive-box dilation and leading-coefficient identification (N4); and the N8 reserve, coefficient-level continuity at zero-noise data. Non-claims (Astra #38 §6 + review v40): (1) no construction of resolution, charts, or Jacobians; (2) no construction of the common analytic neighbourhood or of a normally constant subordinate partition; (3) no unrestricted parameter-dependent normal cutoff theorem from N3; (4) no identification of the selected exponent with the RLCT; (5) no claim that a first-nonzero coefficient exists (cancellation may be total); (6) flatness does not imply the function vanishes; (7) no differentiation of asymptotic expansions (the energy identity is an identity of integrals); (8) no general-observable next-log correction (the assembled energy correction was added afterwards as Headline XLIV); (9) no chart correction after cancellation of the face coefficient (`A ≠ 0` is a hypothesis); (10) no positive-box lower-log closed form beyond `boxCoeff`'s binomial definition; (11) no parity, wall-crossing (N5/N6 not started) or all-orders inverse-log division (N7 NO-GO); (12) no convergence of whole expansions from coefficient continuity; (13) no stochastic assertion and no automatic convergence of normalised empirical noise; (14) no probability-expectation interpretation for signed amplitudes (the correction is a theorem about a normalised ratio of integrals). Reviews: `tide-log/gpt6_fidelity_review_v37..v40.md`; design `tide-log/gpt6_bigpicture_v38.md`.

| **CCXX** | **weighted tube densities — the fibre integration chain carries a density factor (u538; Astra #74 module 3, prerequisite for the resolved-space programme)**: `TubeWeight d` (a nonnegative bounded measurable weight `w`, with `TubeWeight.one` and `TubeWeight.restrict` to a measurable set) is threaded through CCXV–CCXIX: the pulled-back density becomes `tubeDensity T C wt = 1_Ω · tubeJac · (w ∘ ψ)` (measurable via the measurably extended chart `tubeChartM`; `tubeDensity_of_mem/of_notMem`, `tubeDensity_le`), and every statement now reads with `w` in the measure and the observable alone in the Taylor jets: `∫_{U'} w F dy = ∫_z ∫ F∘ψ_z dη^w_z dz` (`integral_tube_piece_fibreMeasure`), the nonnegative and `omegaMeasure` forms, the pushforward density `τ_*(w dy) = C_w(z) dz` (`integral_foot_eq_fibreMass`), finite moments of the weighted fibre measures (`integrable_norm_pow_tubeFibre`, using the bound `tubeJac · bound`), the expansions (`integral_tube_piece_eq_tsum_moment/_multiIndex/_normalContraction`), the normalised measures and `∫_{U'} w F = ∫_{U'} w · cs_w(F)∘proj` (`integral_tube_piece_eq_integral_condContraction`), and the global assembly `∫_U w F = ∫_U w · G_w(F)∘proj` (`integral_tube_eq_integral_globalContraction`) — where the truncation to the pieces is now a **restriction of the weight**, which acts fibrewise on the densities (`tubeDensity_restrict`, `condTubeNormalMeasure_restrict`, `condContractionSeries_restrict`), replacing CCXIX's germ lemma. This is exactly the shape the paper needs on the resolution: cutoffs, cover weights `ρ_i`, the Jacobian unit and the prior live in `w`, and the derivatives act on `φ∘π` alone (Astra #74: "keep cutoffs, cover weights, Jacobian units and prior in the measure"). Non-claims: the weight is required bounded (all resolved-space weights are, on compact chart domains) | TubularFibreIntegration.lean, TubularExpansion.lean, TubularCoordFree.lean, TubularPushforward.lean, TubularGlobalExpansion.lean |
| **CCXXV** | **the leading-coefficient interface over chart–stratum pairs (u543; Astra #74 module 7)**: a **leading-term certificate** `HasLeadingTerm Z c λ k` for a function of the inverse temperature is `Z(N)/(N^{−λ}(log N)^k) → c` (`powLogScale`); the identified-leading-term equivalences of CCV (`∫ F e^{−NK} ~ c N^{−λ}(log N)^{m−1}`) are certificates with `k = m − 1` (`hasLeadingTerm_of_isEquivalent`, no sign hypothesis: for `c = 0` the equivalence forces `Z = 0` eventually), a certificate with `c ≠ 0` is such an equivalence (`HasLeadingTerm.isEquivalent`) and one with `c = 0` is `Z = o(N^{−λ}(log N)^k)` (`HasLeadingTerm.isLittleO`); certificates are linear (`add`, `const_mul`, `sum`, `congr'`); **dominance of exponent pairs** `(λ,k) ≻ (λ',k')` iff `λ < λ'` or `λ = λ', k' < k` makes the dominated scale `o` of the dominating one (`tendsto_powLogScale_div`, via `isLittleO_log_rpow_rpow_atTop` and `log N → ∞`), so a dominated certified term is a certificate with coefficient `0` at the dominating pair (`HasLeadingTerm.of_dominated`); hence **a finite sum of certified terms has the leading term of the extremal pair** — minimal exponent, then maximal log degree among the terms attaining it (`extremalExponent`, `extremalDegree`) — **with coefficient the sum over the tied terms** (`hasLeadingTerm_sum_of_extremal` for any pair below all exponents/above all tied degrees; `hasLeadingTerm_sum_extremal` with the computed extremal pair), cancellation included (a zero sum says the total is `o` of the scale); and for the resolved Boltzmann integral `Z_N[F] = ∫_{⋃ φ_i(dom_i)} F e^{−NK} p dx` as a function of `N` (`boltzmannIntegral`, integrable for `N ≥ 0` from `F·p` integrable and `K ≥ 0`: `integrable_boltzmann`) the chart–stratum decomposition of CCXXIV holds for every `N ≥ 0` (`boltzmannIntegral_eq_sum_pieces`, piece integrals `pieceIntegral` with the explicit chart density `chartDensity = 1_{dom_i}|det Dφ_i|(ρ_i∘Φ_i)(e^{−NK}p)∘Φ_i`), so **certificates for the nonempty chart–stratum pieces plus the vanishing of the divisor-free pieces at the extremal scale give the leading term of `Z_N[F]` with coefficient `∑_{(i,I) tied} c_{i,I}`** (`hasLeadingTerm_boltzmannIntegral`). Non-claims: the per-piece certificates and the extremal pair are hypotheses here (to be discharged from hironaka's monomial data `(e_i, h_i)` on each piece, where CCV/CCVIII give the pair `min_j (h_{n_j}+1)/(2k_j)` and its multiplicity); no positivity of the summed coefficient for signed observables; the divisor-free pieces are assumed `o` of the extremal scale (exponentially small under a positive lower bound of `K∘φ_i`, not proved here) | LeadingTermInterface.lean |
| **CCXXIV** | **the global weighted expansion over resolution charts — the exact resolved-chart formula and the chart–stratum decomposition (u542; Astra #74 module 6)**: for a resolution cover `R` (compact chart domains `dom_i`, `C¹` chart maps `φ_i`, normalised cover weights `ρ_i`) and a bounded measurable target density `c ≥ 0` (typically the Boltzmann density `e^{−NK} p` with `N ≥ 0`, `K ≥ 0` measurable and a bounded prior weight `p`: `TubeWeight.boltzmann`), the **chart weight** `w_i = 1_{dom_i} · |det Dφ_i| · (ρ_i ∘ Φ_i) · (c ∘ Φ_i)` is a bounded measurable `TubeWeight` (`chartWeight`; the Jacobian is bounded on the compact domain, `jacBound`, and `ρ_i ≤ 1`), and against it the chart pullback of any observable reproduces exactly the weighted source measure of `CoverAssembly` (`integral_chartWeight_mul`, with the integrability transfer `integrable_chartWeight_mul`), so **the exact resolved-chart formula** holds: `∫_{⋃ φ_i(dom_i)} F c dx = ∑_i ∫ w_i(y) F(φ_i y) dy` (`integral_union_eq_sum_chartWeight`; Boltzmann form `coverIntegral_eq_sum_chartWeight`: the paper's `Z_N[F] = ∑_i ∫ ρ_i F e^{−NK} p |det Dφ_i| ∘ φ_i`); stratifying each chart by coordinate size (CCXXIII, with a chart-dependent set `D_i` of divisor coordinates and one threshold `ε`) gives **the chart–stratum decomposition** `Z_N[F] = ∑_i (∑_{∅ ≠ I ⊆ D_i} ∫_{piece_{i,I}} w_i F∘φ_i + ∫_{piece_{i,∅}} w_i F∘φ_i)`, exact including the divisor-free pieces (`coverIntegral_eq_sum_chart_pieces`), and under the paper's fibre power-series hypotheses for `F ∘ φ_i` on every nonempty chart–stratum piece (radius, a.e. fibre containment, moment domination — with the Boltzmann chart weight in the fibre measures) **the global weighted expansion** `Z_N[F] = ∑_{(i,I)} ∫_{piece_{i,I}} w_i · (∑_k ⟨D^k_⊥(F∘φ_i), 𝖬^κ_k⟩)(foot_I y) dy + (divisor-free terms)` (`coverIntegral_eq_sum_pieceContraction`; | **XCII** | **independence iff essential face constancy (u383; Astra #48 unit 4)**: `ν^ξ` and the weighted base face measure `μ_P` are mutually absolutely continuous (`faceLocationLimit_absolutelyContinuous`, `faceBaseMeasure_absolutelyContinuous`; `J_λ > 0`), so a `ν^ξ`-a.e. value of the face phase transfers to the box wherever `η(P_J u) w(u) ≠ 0` (`ae_box_of_ae_faceLocation`), which is exactly what the disintegration needs: `ξ = a` `ν^ξ`-a.e. ⇒ `Q̃^ξ = ρ_a ⊗ ν^ξ` (`spatialJointFaceLaw_prod_of_ae`, closing the missing direction of LXXXV/LXXXVI). Fixed-parameter iff `Q̃^ξ = ρ_a ⊗ ν^ξ ↔ ξ = a` `ν^ξ`-a.e. (`spatialJointFaceLaw_prod_iff_ae`, via the strict monotonicity of the tilted mean), and **energy ⊥ face location under `Q̃^ξ` iff the face phase is `ν^ξ`-essentially constant** (`spatialJointFaceLaw_indep_iff`). Note: constancy `μ_P`-a.e. is equivalent here because `μ_P` already carries the amplitude `η` | SpatialIndependenceAE.lean |
| **XCI** | **the random field jointly with a posterior draw (u382; Astra #48 units 1–3)**: a weakly continuous family `Q : P → ProbabilityMeasure Z` (`Z` Polish) is a Markov kernel (`measurable_toMeasure_of_continuous`: open-set masses lower semicontinuous by portmanteau + π–λ; `kernelOfContinuous`), so the marked mixture `μ(dp) Q(p,dz)` is a law on `P × Z` (`jointKernelLaw` via `Measure.compProd`, `integral_jointKernelLaw`). Marked-integral continuity `p_j → p, q_j ⇒ q ⇒ ∫ f(p_j,z) dq_j → ∫ f(p,z) dq` (`tendsto_integral_marked`: tightness of `{q_j}` + uniform continuity on the compact `({p} ∪ {p_j}) × C`) turns continuous convergence of law-valued maps into scalar continuous convergence, and LXXXIX gives the **kernel-law transfer** `μ_n ⇒ μ₀ ⇒ μ_n(dp) Q_n(p,dz) ⇒ μ₀(dp) Q(p,dz)` (`tendsto_jointKernelLaw`). Spatial instantiation: `Q_{N_m}(ext ·) → Q̃(ext ·)` continuously on `C(K,ℝ)` (`continuouslyConverges_posteriorMap`), the limit map is continuous (`continuous_limitPosteriorMap`), and for random phases `X_m ⇒ X` in `C(K,ℝ)` the joint law of the environment with one posterior draw converges, `μ_m(dp) Q_{N_m}(ext p)(dz) ⇒ μ₀(dp) Q̃(ext p)(dz)` on `C(K,ℝ) × ℝ × ℝ^d` (`randomField_posteriorDraw_tendsto`), as does the annealed posterior-draw marginal (`randomField_posteriorDraw_marginal_tendsto`, `integral_jointKernelLaw_snd`). XC is a consequence. Not claimed: original-space posterior; stable convergence w.r.t. an external σ-algebra; convergence in law of the phase field | PosteriorDraw.lean |
| **XC** | **the random-field finite-observable transfer (u381; Astra #47 unit 5)**: for random spatial phases with laws `μ_m ⇒ μ₀` on the Polish phase space `C(K,ℝ)` (external input) and bounded continuous tests `g_1,…,g_r` on `ℝ × ℝ^d`, with `T_m(p) = (E_{Q_{N_m}(ext p)}[g_i])_i` and `T(p) = (E_{Q̃(ext p)}[g_i])_i`, the graph laws converge, `(X_m, T_m(X_m)) ⇒ (X, T(X))` (`randomField_graphLaw_tendsto`): the moving-phase theorem LXXXVIII gives arbitrary-index sequential convergence, the bridge upgrades it to continuous convergence (`phaseObservable_continuouslyConverges`), and LXXXIX transfers. Consequences: the limiting observable map `p ↦ T(p)` is continuous on `C(K,ℝ)` (`continuous_limitObservable`); the observable laws converge, `T_m(X_m) ⇒ T(X)` (`randomField_observable_tendsto`); the mixed annealed identity `∫ φ(p) E_{Q_{N_m}(ext p)}[g_i] dμ_m → ∫ φ(p) E_{Q̃(ext p)}[g_i] dμ₀` for bounded continuous `φ` (`randomField_annealed_tendsto`, clipping the output to `[−‖g_i‖, ‖g_i‖]`). | **LXXXIX** | **the graph-law transfer (u380; Astra #47 unit 4)**: if probability laws `μ_n ⇒ μ₀` on `P` are uniformly tight and measurable `T_n : P → V` converge continuously to `T`, the graph laws converge, `(id, T_n)_# μ_n ⇒ (id, T)_# μ₀` (`tendsto_graphLaw_of_continuouslyConverges`; `V` pseudo-metric second countable): the fixed-map CMT handles `f_∞(p) = f(p, T p)`, and under the same `μ_n`, `|∫ f_n dμ_n − ∫ f_∞ dμ_n| ≤ sup_C|f_n − f_∞| + 2‖f‖ μ_n(Cᶜ)` (`abs_integral_sub_le_of_close_on`) with `f_n → f_∞` uniformly on the compact `C` by LXXXVIII's compact-uniform lemma. Not claimed: any extended CMT beyond graph laws of continuously convergent maps | GraphLawTransfer.lean |
| — | **continuous convergence API (u379; Astra #47 unit 3)**: `ContinuouslyConverges Tn T := ∀ p, Tendsto (fun q : ℕ × P => Tn q.1 q.2) (atTop ×ˢ 𝓝 p) (𝓝 (T p))`; arbitrary-index sequential consequence (`tendsto_seq`, `tendsto_pointwise`); the sequential bridge on a pseudo-metric domain (`continuouslyConverges_of_seq`, by contradiction with `κ_j ≥ j`, `d(x_j,p) < 1/(j+1)`); continuity of the limit for a pseudo-metric codomain (`continuous_limit`); uniform convergence on compact sets by finite subcover (`tendstoUniformlyOn`) — no sequential-compactness machinery | ContinuousConvergence.lean |
| **LXXXVIII** | **moving-phase joint weak convergence and phase-continuity of the limit law (u377; Astra #47 unit 1)**: for `N_m → ∞` and measurable phases with `‖ξ_m − ξ₀‖_∞ ≤ ε_m → 0` on the box (`ξ₀` continuous, `η ≥ 0` continuous, positive face weight), the joint law of `(N_m K, u)` under `Q_{N_m}^{ξ_m}` converges weakly in `ProbabilityMeasure (ℝ × ℝ^d)` to the limiting joint law `Q̃^{ξ₀}` (`movingPhaseJointLaw_tendsto`): per bounded continuous test `g`, the moving-phase expectation is the fixed-phase one plus the LXXVI perturbation with observable `u ↦ g(N_m K(u), u)` bounded by `‖g‖`, and the fixed-phase law converges by LXXXII (`phaseJointLaw_integral`, `phaseJointLaw_isProbabilityMeasure_of_near`). | — | **the face-zero part of the second coefficient functional is the log-weighted face integral (u364; Astra #45 unit 2, step 4)**: with the logarithmic face weight `L(u) = ∑_{ℓ∉J} 2k_ℓ log u_ℓ` (`logWeight`, `≤ 0` on the box) and `∫₀¹ t^{a−1} log t dt = −1/a²` (`integral_Ioc_rpow_mul_log`), termwise `∫ u^γ(P_J u) w(u) L(u) du = −[γ_J = 0] (∏_{i∉J} 1/aᵢ) ∑_{ℓ∉J} 2k_ℓ/a_ℓ`, `aᵢ = hᵢ+γᵢ+1−2kᵢλ` (`integral_mono_faceProj_residualWeight_logWeight`), which is exactly `K_k` times the face-zero second coefficient of `u^{h+γ}` (`coeff_second_faceZero_eq_integral`, via `prod_inv_two_k_mul_residualProd`, `residualSum_eq`); summed over an absolutely summable family (dominated by `|f_γ| w (−L)`): `K_k ∑_γ f_γ [γ_J=0] c^{(2)}(h+γ) = (1/((m−2)! ∏_{i∈J} 2kᵢ)) ∫ f(P_J u) w(u) L(u) du` (`coeffFunctional_second_faceZero_eq`) | FaceFunctionalLog.lean |

## Programme P — the §3 population theorem rewritten around the Taylor tree (opened 2026-09-08; Astra #37; cap 20 units, reviews after units 3/5/9/12/16/20)
User instruction (2026-09-08): consult Astra on the correct path for §3 "based on what we are trying to do, and attempt to formalise that". Astra #37 (`tide-log/gpt6_bigpicture_v37.md`): GO for a staged conditional population theorem ("Population expansions from admissible normal-form data", Theorem A/Corollary B; staging note `projects/grammar/staging/sec3-rewrite-note.md`), NO-GO for declaring the present geometric §3 theorem proved. Corrections to the paper: candidate exponents ≠ nonzero leading terms; the leading coefficient lives on the minimal-ratio face (corner value in the all-minimal case; in general the face integral is essential); after `h ↦ h+l` the amplitude is the residual `ψ`, not `u^l ψ`; `a_{-m}` depends on the cutoff `b` through nonminimal coordinates; a finite normal Taylor degree is not an asymptotic-order cutoff; parity is governed by the amplitude monomial; wall-crossing demoted to a remark. Steps 1–3 (change of variables, localisation, adapted partition of unity) and analytic admissibility of the localised amplitudes remain hypotheses.

**★ PROGRAMME P RELEASE (2026-09-09; units 290–305 + audit; reviews v32–v36 all PASS; Astra #37 design). Release paragraph (review v36 wording):** Programme P establishes the rewritten leading-order population normal-form results of §3. For the stated monomial-chart hypotheses it identifies the leading power–log scale and its face-functional coefficient, transfers these results through the supported tangential integration and finite-chart assembly, and obtains asymptotic equivalences when the relevant leading coefficient is nonzero. For observable insertions with actual nonzero leading terms it proves the leading expectation quotient and its three admissible regimes; boundedness relative to a common nonnegative base integrand excludes a numerator that precedes the denominator. For the energy observable `K` it proves the exact face-coefficient shift `A_K = (λ/β) A` and hence `N E_N[K] → λ/β`, with the assembled conclusion `N E_N[K] → μ_*/β`. Construction of the geometric normal forms, identification with the original model and its RLCT, and the external assembly decompositions and residual estimates remain hypotheses; these are leading-order population results, not a full higher-order or noisy empirical expansion.
**Non-claims (reproduce in the author note):** (1) no general full asymptotic series beyond the stated cutoff remainders; (2) no differentiation of asymptotic equivalents — no partition-function derivative identity, no `1/(N log N)` energy correction; (3) no next-leading-term recovery after cancellation; (4) no observable-order rule from divisor orders alone — the quotient classification assumes actual nonzero leading terms; (5) order exclusion needs a bounded quotient (bounded observable + common nonnegative base integrand); (6) signed amplitudes have no automatic probability interpretation; (7) no construction of the external geometry or global decomposition (original-model identification, chart validity, outside-chart controls external); (8) the energy residual is not controlled by the denominator residual; (9) chart hypotheses (`kᵢ > 0`, one-sided unit boxes, continuity/holomorphy or ℓ¹ admissibility) are not removed; (10) nothing stochastic or empirical follows from the population tranche. **Label rule for the mirror:** label the proved restricted statement and spell out the restriction next to the label; `thm:expectation_expansion` → "leading-order population normal-form version"; `eq:thm_leading_coeff` → the face functional with its normalisation; `eq:lambda_I_f` → the shifted first CANDIDATE (u295), not an actual leading exponent; `eq:mu_I_phi` → NOT attached to u302/u303; `eq:expectation_leading` → u302 (+ u303 order constraint); `ex:phi_equals_K` → u304/u305, leading limit only.

Review v32 (u290–292): PASS ×3 — "correct zero-phase specialization, explicitly connected to the original population integral, preserving the entire TaylorTreeConclusion"; wording fixes applied (real-part agreement, finite `q`-range `j ≤ q ≤ n`, `cη_γ = η_γ/γ!` normalisation, canonical-coefficient hypotheses). Review v33 (u293–294): PASS ×2 — "implement route A–D faithfully and complete the stated P1 leading-coefficient identification"; wording fixes applied ("any admissible Taylor-tree coefficient system" rather than "canonical"; ambient continuity of `η`; corner evaluation in the all-minimal case, face integral essential in general). P1 non-claims: `λ` is not shown to be the first exponent with a NONZERO coefficient; no formulas for `C(λ,j)`, `j < m−1`; nothing about `C(λ,j)` for `j > n`; no uniqueness of the whole coefficient system; unit box only, no chart sum.

**★★ SEABED COMPLETE — CONDITIONAL §4 RELEASE (2026-09-08; Astra #36 wording).** The Lean formalisation, with no `sorry`, establishes the fluctuation-function and ladder-algebra results, the Taylor-tree expansion in every positive normal dimension under the declared analytic hypotheses, and a continuous-coefficient finite-cutoff stochastic extension through tangential integration and finite chart assembly. It also establishes the leading-order posterior quotient under joint data convergence, source predecessor cancellation, negligible decomposition residuals, and an almost-surely nonzero limiting denominator coefficient. The stochastic results are conditional on convergence in the stated weighted-ℓ¹/tangential-data topologies and on an externally supplied finite-chart decomposition; they do not derive those inputs from Hypothesis I, construct a resolution atlas, or identify a Gaussian empirical-process limit. All-orders posterior division and the population expansion in the unrevised §3 remain outside this release. The normal-block, Taylor-tree, and stochastic Taylor-tree reports (`projects/grammar/staging/`) constitute the mathematical hand-off. "Complete" is a release decision about a precisely delimited scope, not a claim that every paper label has a Lean counterpart. Headlines I–XXXVIII, units 1–289, reviews v1–v31.

## Completion statement (2026-09-07)
The scoped **§4 normal-block programme is complete** at the reviewed baseline `d65d48b` (Astra #24–#25; reviews
v1–v17): general machinery (Headlines VI–XIX), the assembled statistical example (XX–XX'' with the genuine-prior
lemmas), and the Abelian coefficient dictionary (XXI). Explicit non-claims: XX–XX'' are fixed-`θ` moment-generating-
function limits, not a formalised weak-convergence theorem for posterior laws; XXI is a real-axis Abelian coefficient
limit, not meromorphic continuation or an exact-pole-order theorem; the signed-weight statements are analytic, the
posterior interpretation needs `ρ ≥ 0`. The remaining paper material (full Taylor-tree expansion, complex Mellin
continuation, the boundary tail, `eq:flucttreeterms`, the resolution-based §4.3 application) is **outside the
completed work package**. Preferred separately authorised successor (Astra #25): posterior weak convergence of the
law of `√n x₀x₁` to `N(z,1)` for deterministic phases (reconnaissance first: Lévy/Curtiss route vs direct
test-function route); a cheap robustness variant is available via Mathlib's CLT (i.i.d. mean-0 variance-1 data).

Pinned commit for the mirror `grammar_lean.tex`: see `\laplaceLeanCommit` there. All statements
zero `sorry`/`axiom`; independent statement-level reviews v1–v15 in `tide-log/gpt6_fidelity_review_v*.md`.
Conventions: boxes `(0,1]^d` (`unitBox`), `(-1,1]^d` (`symBox`); `ratioExp h k i = (hᵢ+1)/(2kᵢ)`,
`λ = min`, `J` the minimisers, `m = multCount`; chart variable `N` (paper's `√n`), `p = 2λ`;
`phaseMoment β p a = ∫₀^∞ s^{p-1} e^{-βs²+βas} ds` is the paper's `J_p(a) = S_{p/2}(a)/2`.

## What the theorems assume (hand-off)
See `projects/grammar/staging/normal-block-report-v3.pdf` §Assumptions: the chart decomposition is an
external input ("conditional" = assuming); densities are deterministic and strictly positive; no
remainders; joint convergence of the empirical phases is a hypothesis; the stochastic results are weak
limits, not expansions with rates; selection is at the normalised scale and signed numerators may cancel.
Headlines XX/XX'/XX'' concern one concrete statistical model with no chart-decomposition or likelihood-remainder
hypothesis (XX assumes `Zₙ → z`; XX'/XX'' assume i.i.d. `N(0,1)` data). They are fixed-`θ` moment-generating-function
limits, not weak convergence of the posterior law, with no rate; the weight `ρ` may be signed (`ρ(0) > 0` only), and for a
genuine prior (`ρ ≥ 0` on the box) the evidence is positive at every sample size (`NormalCrossingPrior.lean`). Reviews v16–v17.
